Dress the dining table more festively: a sprig of fir and a few small pinecones, two or three small red and pewter-silver glass baubles, a short cream candle, a soft warm string light trailing across the matte table surface, all at the edges of frame so the box stays the clear subject.

Shot on an iPhone 16 Pro, photorealistic, extremely high quality, tack-sharp focus on the box and the hands, crisp legible detail in the printing on the carton, perfect natural realistic lighting, perfectly exposed, hyper realistic, zero motion blur, nothing blurry or soft, no CGI look. The wordmark printed on the red band must read exactly THEXMASGLOW - eleven letters, T-H-E-X-M-A-S-G-L-O-W, correctly spelled, sharp and legible, with no invented or misspelled brand name anywhere on the carton.

### Image prompt
````text
Recreate @Image 1 with two changes.

KEEP IDENTICAL: the same vertical 9:16 iPhone photo, the same dim hallway, the same antique brass round doorknob in the same place, the same small pine wreath with the red velvet bow hung on the door, the same oak floorboards, the same woman's forearm entering from the lower right with the heather-grey Aran-knit wool sleeve and no jewellery, with fair pale skin with pink undertones, slender adult woman's fingers and short natural nails, her hand closed around the knob, the same camera position and camera height, the same warm low-key evening light.

CHANGE 1 - THE DOOR. It is now a Williamsburg-blue painted six-panel Colonial door with raised panels, a satin finish and crisp white-painted surrounding trim, an antique brass round knob and matching brass escutcheon, set in a cream painted plaster wall. Same size, same position in frame, same flat-on angle as @Image 1.

CHANGE 2 - the door is FULLY CLOSED and flush in its frame. There is NO gap at the edge of the door and absolutely NO light spilling out - no rainbow strip, no coloured glow, no bright line, no glare anywhere along the door edge, the jamb, the threshold or the floor. The seam between door and frame is a plain dark shadow line only. Nothing behind the door is visible or lit.

THE WREATH - it hangs on the FACE of the door, centred on the upper half of the door and comfortably inside the frame with clear door surface visible all the way around it. It must NOT be cut off by the right edge of the picture and must NOT drift onto the door frame or the wall. The whole circle of the wreath and the whole red velvet bow are visible.

EXPOSURE - brighter and warmer than @Image 1, which came out too dark. The hallway is dim but comfortably readable: the Williamsburg-blue paint reads clearly as soft slate blue, the brass knob catches a warm highlight, the oak floorboards and the forearm are clearly lit, and the shadows stay deep but still hold detail. Do not crush the blacks and do not let any part of the frame go to a flat dark mass.

SHARPNESS - this frame must be tack sharp edge to edge: every moulding edge and panel corner in the door, the machined edge and the patina of the brass knob, the individual needles of the pine wreath and the woven nap of the red velvet bow, the skin texture and fine hairs on the forearm, the grain of the floorboards. Extremely high quality, perfect lighting, perfectly exposed, hyper realistic. BANNED: blur, soft focus, haze, mushy low-detail surfaces, waxy skin, a dreamy or filtered look, motion blur, noise, upscaling artefacts.

Natural handheld UGC look, shot on an iPhone 16 Pro, photorealistic, zero motion blur, no CGI look.

No text, no captions, no watermarks, no lettering anywhere in the image.

REFERENCE ROLES: @Image 1 owns the exact layout, product geometry, hand positions and camera. @Image 2 owns the established photographic look and product fidelity only; use the new hands, materials and background specified above. ## C05 F2 - The door opens on the room — room reference
**Frame:** `references/v25_c05_r1.png`
**Settings:** gpt_image_2_5 · sunburst · 9:16 · 2k · medium · 1520×2688 · count 1 · media role image → image_references (backend)
**Job ID:** `a022fcb7-c595-45e1-a20c-cc1cf85b4185`
**References:**
- @Image 1: `content/video_11_assets/references/v11_c05_r1.png` · Higgsfield media `dda4701e-8d9d-4351-99f8-60bb9093cf68`
- @Image 2: `content/video_9_assets/references/v9_c05_r4.png` · Higgsfield media `1c500339-2855-45b6-bd9b-4c3633b5a272`
**Lineage:** Reused from v24 C05 r1 - changed: set nouns only (Early-American Colonial; hands, sweater, table/counter/kitchen, door, children, pyjamas, room, fold sentences, projection theme as applicable).

### Image prompt
````text
Recreate @Image 1's camera, framing, scale and poses. Wholesome family Christmas scene. @Image 1 owns composition geometry only. @Image 2 owns the dense saturated photographic projection look; change the architecture and children to the descriptions below. Both reference images are generated stills, not photographs of a documented real house.

KEEP IDENTICAL: the same vertical 9:16 iPhone photo look, the same camera position and camera height, the same wide framing of a whole tall room seen from a doorway, the same natural low-key evening light in which the only light in the room is the projection itself, and the same two children - a boy standing TURNED AROUND FACING THE CAMERA, front-on, his face fully visible and lit by the coloured light, looking towards the lens with a delighted open-mouthed smile, arms slightly out in wonder, and a girl beside him seen from behind, still looking up at the projected wall.

The boy is of about seven with fair skin and sandy-blond hair in a neat side part; the girl is of about four with fair rosy skin and light-brown hair in two little French braids. Both children are fully dressed for winter in red-and-green Christmas tartan brushed-cotton pyjama sets with cream piping, with matching loose pyjama trousers and cream wool socks. Wholesome family Christmas scene.

CHANGE 1 - THE ROOM. Build a structurally believable room: a spacious double-height early-American Colonial Christmas great room with a high CATHEDRAL CEILING: two broad white plaster planes rise to a crisp central ridge, crossed by four honey-toned hand-hewn chestnut tie beams. Williamsburg blue-grey painted raised panelling lines the lower walls with white plaster above. A tall chimney breast of old red brick stands on the back wall with a white-painted wooden mantel; a wall of tall twelve-over-twelve sash windows with white-painted wooden frames and muntins occupies the left wall and a wide white-painted panelled doorway sits on the right. Wide heart-pine floorboards, a large oatmeal linen slipcovered sectional sofa, a round cognac leather ottoman and a red-and-cream braided wool rug. Seen from the doorway; the new a Williamsburg-blue painted six-panel Colonial door with raised panels, a satin finish and crisp white-painted surrounding trim is open out of frame.

It is night, all house lamps switched off. A real Christmas tree with warm gold lights, red, cream and pewter-silver glass baubles, tinsel and wrapped gifts stands beside the fireplace. Evergreen garland with holly berries covers the mantel, with three red-and-white knitted stockings below. The projection is the main illumination.

CHANGE 2 - THE PROJECTION. ONE continuous Christmas panorama covers every visible wall and the entire ceiling, including the whole ceiling, every architectural plane and seam, every beam or cove, and the entire chimney breast all the way to the ceiling. No rectangle, no screen, no blank ceiling or wall patch. Architectural occlusion shadows remain real and narrow. LOW ALONG THE WALLS, a SNOWY ENCHANTED CHRISTMAS FOREST: tall snow-laden fir trees hung with glowing lanterns, a herd of reindeer trotting between the trunks, a red fox family and snowshoe hares darting across the snowdrifts, a little log cabin with glowing windows and a curl of white smoke rising from its chimney, and a frozen stream winding through the trees. Along the upper walls, strings of golden lights, red velvet bows and dense emerald garlands swag from tree to tree into a continuous Christmas panorama, with big soft snowflakes drifting down through the branches. ACROSS THE CEILING, Santa's red sleigh and eight reindeer fly in a wide arc past a big silver full moon, among twinkling golden stars and shooting stars, a long sparkling golden trail streaming behind them. Unmistakably Christmas Eve, glowing gold, cranberry red and deep forest green dominant with snow-white accents against a near-black midnight sky. One dense continuous panorama of projected light across the architecture, no isolated sticker icons, no carnival, no galaxy, no vortex. No projected shape floats physically in the room. AND THE NIGHT SKY BETWEEN THE ELEMENTS IS DEEP AND DARK: everywhere the picture is not occupied by a lit element it is a rich near-black midnight sky of blackened indigo and very dark crimson, NOT white paint, NOT pale drywall and NOT a grey haze. It is that darkness between the elements that makes the bright ones burn.  HOW THE LIGHT SITS ON THE SURFACES, and this has to be exactly right. THE PROJECTION IS DENSE AND ROUGHLY NINETY PERCENT OPAQUE, and it is the brightest and most saturated thing in the frame. It lies on the room like an enormous backlit stained-glass window: deep, dense, fully saturated colour at full strength, crimson and ruby and warm gold and emerald at their richest, and it OVERPOWERS the colour of the surface underneath completely.  The material colour is overpowered by the projected colour; only surface texture and physical shadows remain legible through it. It is NOT pale, NOT pastel, NOT milky, NOT hazy, NOT semi-transparent, NOT a faint tint and NOT a washed-out overlay; there is no grey veil over any of it, and no part of the projection is so faint that the bare surface shows through as white.  AND IT WRAPS THE ARCHITECTURE. ONE SINGLE PROJECTOR sitting low in the near corner of the room throws all of this from that one point, so the entire picture keystones away from that origin and every surface takes it at its own angle. This is real projected light crossing a real three-dimensional room, so it obeys every surface it lands on.   The two sloping ceiling planes and their central ridge remain clearly three-dimensional, meeting at a crisp ridge line. The sleigh and the ceiling picture fold at the ridge and are foreshortened along each slope, running straight across the faces of the four chestnut tie beams, before continuing onto the walls. The tall brick chimney breast receives a continuous picture with clear folds at its projecting corners. The painted raised panelling, the white-painted sash window frames and muntins and the panelled doorway frame carry the projection straight across their faces, with thin shadow breaks only at their actual edges. Bright imagery follows the built curves and planes, never a flat picture hovering across the room. All raised trim casts a real hard-edged shadow behind it. THE WOODEN FRAMES CARRY THE PICTURE TOO: the painted wooden window frames, sashes and glazing bars, the door and doorway casings, the skirting boards, the mantel and every other piece of wooden trim the beam crosses are covered by the same continuous projected imagery as the wall beside them - never left as bare white unlit outlines - with only a thin real shadow line at each edge. THIS IS THE KEY CORRECTION FOR THIS SET: the exposed wood must visibly carry the picture. Every exposed piece of wood in shot - each beam, rafter, truss, brace, panel, post and rail, window frame, mullion, sash, muntin and glazing bar, the door and doorway casings, the skirting and the mantel - is painted over by the projection in the same full colour as the plaster beside it: the forest, lanterns, garlands, stars and snowflakes run straight across the face of the wood and bend at each edge, the wood grain showing faintly through the projected colour. No beam, frame or casing stays as a dark bare wooden stripe or an unlit outline. Only the window GLASS stays the dark night outside. There is NO falloff anywhere. The picture stays strong, dense and fully coloured right into all four corners of the frame and right up into the corners of the ceiling, with no dim zone, no dark gap, no faded edge and no bare patch at any boundary of the room.  EVERY ELEMENT OF THE PROJECTION IS RAZOR SHARP: every elf, toy, ribbon, nutcracker detail, antler, snowflake, fir needle and window has precise edges. The thousands of points of light are DISCRETE PINPOINTS - tiny, tight, individually separated dots - and not soft round blobs, not fuzzy balls and not smeared streaks. There is NO bloom, NO glow halo, NO lens flare, NO atmospheric haze, NO soft-focus glaze and NO gaussian softness anywhere over the picture, and the brightest cores stay tight and do not bleed into what surrounds them. The whole frame is in focus from the nearest person to the farthest corner of the ceiling: nothing is out of focus, nothing is soft, and there is no depth-of-field blur and no bokeh anywhere in the image.

CHANGE 3 - NOTHING IS PROJECTED ON THE FLOOR. The projected imagery lives on the WALLS and the CEILING only. The heart-pine floorboards, the red-and-cream braided wool rug, the oatmeal linen slipcovered sectional sofa, the round cognac leather ottoman, the tree and the gifts carry NO projected shapes at all - no snowflakes, no stars, no village, no Christmas figures, no gift village, no star, no sleigh, no falling light on them - they are lit only by the soft warm colour spill coming off the walls and sit in gentle shadow. Both children's faces and their cotton pyjamas catch that spill so both faces read clearly.

NATURAL, LESS CINEMATIC LOOK - one small notch more natural than a polished film still: this is a candid photo a parent snapped on an iPhone at home, not a movie frame, a poster, a painting or an advert. ACROSS THE CEILING, Santa's red sleigh and eight reindeer fly in a wide arc past a big silver full moon, among twinkling golden stars and shooting stars, a long sparkling golden trail streaming behind them. Unmistakably Christmas Eve, glowing gold, cranberry red and deep forest green dominant with snow-white accents against a near-black midnight sky. One dense continuous panorama of projected light across the architecture, no isolated sticker icons, no carnival, no galaxy, no vortex. No projected shape floats physically in the room. AND THE NIGHT SKY BETWEEN THE ELEMENTS IS DEEP AND DARK: everywhere the picture is not occupied by a lit element it is a rich near-black midnight sky of blackened indigo and very dark crimson, NOT white paint, NOT pale drywall and NOT a grey haze. It is that darkness between the elements that makes the bright ones burn.  HOW THE LIGHT SITS ON THE SURFACES, and this has to be exactly right. THE PROJECTION IS DENSE AND ROUGHLY NINETY PERCENT OPAQUE, and it is the brightest and most saturated thing in the frame. It lies on the room like an enormous backlit stained-glass window: deep, dense, fully saturated colour at full strength, crimson and ruby and warm gold and emerald at their richest, and it OVERPOWERS the colour of the surface underneath completely.  The material colour is overpowered by the projected colour; only surface texture and physical shadows remain legible through it. It is NOT pale, NOT pastel, NOT milky, NOT hazy, NOT semi-transparent, NOT a faint tint and NOT a washed-out overlay; there is no grey veil over any of it, and no part of the projection is so faint that the bare surface shows through as white.  AND IT WRAPS THE ARCHITECTURE. ONE SINGLE PROJECTOR sitting low in the near corner of the room throws all of this from that one point, so the entire picture keystones away from that origin and every surface takes it at its own angle. This is real projected light crossing a real three-dimensional room, so it obeys every surface it lands on.   The two sloping ceiling planes and their central ridge remain clearly three-dimensional, meeting at a crisp ridge line. The sleigh and the ceiling picture fold at the ridge and are foreshortened along each slope, running straight across the faces of the four chestnut tie beams, before continuing onto the walls. The tall brick chimney breast receives a continuous picture with clear folds at its projecting corners. The painted raised panelling, the white-painted sash window frames and muntins and the panelled doorway frame carry the projection straight across their faces, with thin shadow breaks only at their actual edges. Bright imagery follows the built curves and planes, never a flat picture hovering across the room. All raised trim casts a real hard-edged shadow behind it. THE WOODEN FRAMES CARRY THE PICTURE TOO: the painted wooden window frames, sashes and glazing bars, the door and doorway casings, the skirting boards, the mantel and every other piece of wooden trim the beam crosses are covered by the same continuous projected imagery as the wall beside them - never left as bare white unlit outlines - with only a thin real shadow line at each edge. THIS IS THE KEY CORRECTION FOR THIS SET: the exposed wood must visibly carry the picture. Every exposed piece of wood in shot - each beam, rafter, truss, brace, panel, post and rail, window frame, mullion, sash, muntin and glazing bar, the door and doorway casings, the skirting and the mantel - is painted over by the projection in the same full colour as the plaster beside it: the forest, lanterns, garlands, stars and snowflakes run straight across the face of the wood and bend at each edge, the wood grain showing faintly through the projected colour. No beam, frame or casing stays as a dark bare wooden stripe or an unlit outline. Only the window GLASS stays the dark night outside. There is NO falloff anywhere. The picture stays strong, dense and fully coloured right into all four corners of the frame and right up into the corners of the ceiling, with no dim zone, no dark gap, no faded edge and no bare patch at any boundary of the room.  EVERY ELEMENT OF THE PROJECTION IS RAZOR SHARP: every elf, toy, ribbon, nutcracker detail, antler, snowflake, fir needle and window has precise edges. The thousands of points of light are DISCRETE PINPOINTS - tiny, tight, individually separated dots - and not soft round blobs, not fuzzy balls and not smeared streaks. There is NO bloom, NO glow halo, NO lens flare, NO atmospheric haze, NO soft-focus glaze and NO gaussian softness anywhere over the picture, and the brightest cores stay tight and do not bleed into what surrounds them. The whole frame is in focus from the nearest person to the farthest corner of the ceiling: nothing is out of focus, nothing is soft, and there is no depth-of-field blur and no bokeh anywhere in the image.

CHANGE 3 - NOTHING IS PROJECTED ON THE FLOOR. The projected imagery lives on the WALLS and the CEILING only. The heart-pine floorboards, the red-and-cream braided wool rug, the oatmeal linen slipcovered sectional sofa, the round cognac leather ottoman, the tree and the gifts carry NO projected shapes at all - no snowflakes, no stars, no village, no Christmas figures, no gift village, no star, no sleigh, no falling light on them - they are lit only by the soft warm colour spill coming off the walls and sit in gentle shadow. Both children's faces and their cotton pyjamas catch that spill so both faces read clearly.

CHANGE 4 - the projector unit must NOT be visible. No projector, no side table, no stool, no glowing device anywhere in frame. The corner it would occupy is plain floor and the edge of the rug.

NATURAL, LESS CINEMATIC LOOK - one small notch more natural than a polished film still: this is a candid photo a parent snapped on an iPhone at home, not a movie frame, a poster, a painting or an advert. True-to-life colour and contrast exactly as they come straight out of the phone camera: no colour grading, no teal-and-orange grade, no vignette, no dramatic spotlighting, no heightened fantasy glow on the children, and no storybook-illustration or oil-painting finish on the projection. The projection is still dense, bright and colourful, but its colour sits a touch closer to real life - rich, not neon - and it reads as the real light of a home Christmas projector falling on real walls: the plaster texture and wood grain show faintly through it, and the shadows keep a little more ordinary room detail, with the furniture, rug and floor clearly readable in the soft spill.

Vertical 9:16 photo, shot on an iPhone 16 Pro, natural handheld UGC look, extremely high quality, RAZOR SHARP EDGE TO EDGE AND CORNER TO CORNER at full 2K resolution with fine micro-detail legible everywhere - the fine cotton weave of the pyjamas, the grain of the floorboards, the tooth of the drywall, the grain of the chestnut tie beams and every individual pinpoint of the projection all crisply resolved - perfect lighting, perfectly exposed, hyper realistic, zero motion blur, zero depth-of-field blur, zero bokeh, zero bloom, zero glow haze, zero soft focus, nothing blurry, nothing smeared and nothing soft or gauzy anywhere in the frame, no CGI look. Normal wide-angle lens, straight vertical lines, no fisheye distortion.

No text, no captions, no watermarks, no lettering anywhere in the image.
````

THE CONNECTOR - a white plastic USB-C plug on a white cable, held between the right thumb and forefinger. The white cable trails away to the right across the marble and stays slack. THE LEFT HAND stays still on the dome.
1.2-2.0s - THE CONNECTOR is now fully seated and stays seated for the rest of the clip; it never comes back out. A thin RED light ring ignites in the seam between the white dome and the white stand base and glows steadily.
2.0-3.0s - the BLACK perforated mesh disc on top of the dome fills with a dense field of small RED points of light, like a red starfield, brightening from the centre outward across the black disc. A soft RED glow spills onto the marble around the base of the stand.
3.0-4.0s - the RED light settles and holds at full brightness; the small red points shimmer very slightly. THE RIGHT HAND lets go of the seated plug - which stays in THE PORT - and relaxes onto the counter beside the base. Nothing else moves.

[9] CAMERA AND OPTICS - one absolutely locked-off shot, vertical 9:16, normal wide-angle phone lens, close on the product over the counter. FREEZE MEANS FREEZE: no drift, no wobble, no micro-motion, no push-in, no handheld shake, no reframe, no zoom, no cut. Normal wide-angle only - no fisheye, no barrel distortion.

[10] LIGHTING - warm white under-cabinet kitchen light for the whole clip; the only lighting change is the RED light emitted by THE PROJECTOR itself from 1.0s onward. The red light is a local glow on the product and the marble immediately around it - it does not repaint the whole kitchen.

[11] PHYSICS - THE PROJECTOR is a rigid object bolted to the spot; it does not shift, rock, rotate or lift when the cable is pushed in. The cable is flexible and bends slightly as it seats.

[12] AUDIO - natural room sound only: a small plastic click as the connector seats. No music, no speech, no score.

[13] POSITIVE LOCKS - THE CONNECTOR ends the clip FULLY INSERTED into THE PORT, its metal tongue hidden inside the dome and its white shoulder flush against the white rim; anyone watching can see the plug go into the hole. The light is RED and only RED. The top face of THE PROJECTOR is BLACK and the red starfield appears ON that black disc. The countertop and the kitchen behind it are visible in every single frame. Exactly two hands. The camera never moves.
BANNED: a plug that stops short of the socket; a plug that never moves; a plug pressed against the outside of the dome or the stand instead of entering the socket; a plug pushed into the stand, the base tray, the back or the top instead of the rim socket; a plug that goes in and then comes back out; a plug left hovering in mid-air at the end; a hand that only holds or fiddles with the cable; THE PORT closing over, healing, vanishing or moving to another part of the product; a second socket appearing; blue light, white light, green light, amber light, multicoloured or rainbow light; a beam projected onto the ceiling or the walls; the product moving, lifting or rotating; the camera moving in any way; an all-white top face; the countertop or the kitchen disappearing; a third hand; a cut; subtitles; on-screen text; music.

PRODUCT SCALE LOCK: preserve the exact large full-size projector and its size relative to the adult hands in the supplied start frame. Never shrink it to a miniature; the black perforated mesh top, rigid white body and separate four-foot stand keep their proportions.

Only the projected picture is animated; the room and the children stay real photographic phone footage. Every drawn element below moves with big, bold, easy-to-read motion at normal playback speed, in EVERY frame from the first to the last, independent of the camera. Only the drawn buildings, trees and the moon stay put; everything else in the picture travels.
- SANTA'S SLEIGH AND EIGHT REINDEER: the WHOLE TEAM TRAVELS across the ceiling - it is never parked with only its legs moving. It flies in one smooth sweeping arc from where it is first seen across the cathedral ceiling toward the far side, covering about one quarter of the ceiling's width every second, so the sleigh is in a clearly different place every second and has crossed most of the ceiling by the end. The reindeer's legs gallop, heads bob, the sleigh rocks and dips, and the golden trail streams and curls behind it.
- FOREST LIFE: the herd of reindeer trots steadily between the tree trunks along the wall, legs moving and heads bobbing, travelling about one tree-width every second; the red foxes trot and pounce playfully in the snow; the snowshoe hares hop across the drifts; the snow-laden fir branches sway and shed little puffs of snow; the glowing lanterns sway gently on their branches and flicker; light glints travel along the frozen stream.
- CHIMNEY SMOKE: a tall soft white smoke plume streams continuously out of the log cabin's chimney, rising in a long wavy column up the wall, swaying and curling, each puff climbing about one cabin height every second, widening and fading as fresh puffs push up from below.
- SNOWFALL: big projected snowflakes drift steadily down across the whole picture, about a quarter of the wall height every second, turning as they fall - flat light on the wall and ceiling surfaces, never real snow in the room.
- SKY AND LIGHTS: the silver full moon glows; golden stars twinkle; now and then a shooting star streaks across the ceiling; bright gold sparkle waves travel along the garlands and ribbons.
PER-SECOND ANIMATION TRACK (5 s):
0-1s: the door opens onto the room; through the very first gap the sleigh is already flying and the reindeer herd is already trotting between the trees and smoke is already rising from the cabin chimney.
1-2s: the sleigh team travels across the cathedral ceiling, clearly changing position, reindeer galloping; the reindeer herd trots on between the trunks; the cabin smoke climbs the wall; the foxes pounce; snow drifts down.
2-3s: the sleigh has flown on another quarter of the ceiling; the hares hop across the drifts; the lanterns sway; the fir branches shed puffs of snow.
3-4s: the sleigh keeps flying toward the far side of the ceiling; new smoke puffs rise from the cabin chimney; the reindeer herd keeps trotting; a shooting star streaks across.
4-5s: all of it still moving at the same pace in the final frame - sleigh flying, reindeer herd trotting, cabin smoke rising, lanterns swaying, snow falling.
All of this is flat projected light lying on the real plaster, the chestnut tie beams, the painted panelling and the sash window frames: it bends with the surfaces, never floats into the room, and nothing is projected on the floor, furniture or children.

CHILDREN - SPELLBOUND BY THE CEILING AND ALWAYS MOVING. They keep moving the whole time and follow the sleigh with their eyes, arms and feet. Per second:
0-1s: in the very first gap THE BOY is already walking slowly backwards across the rug, head tipped far back, one arm stretched straight up pointing at Santa's flying sleigh; THE GIRL, a few steps away from him, is up on tiptoe with both hands pressed to her cheeks, mouth open, staring straight up.
1-2s: THE BOY keeps stepping backwards and turns his body to follow the sleigh as it flies overhead, his pointing arm tracking it across the ceiling; THE GIRL runs three quick little steps across the rug in the same direction the sleigh is flying, one hand reaching up toward it.
2-3s: THE BOY looks over at THE GIRL, points up again and shouts with excitement, then looks straight back up; THE GIRL spins round once on the rug with her arms out and her face turned up to the ceiling, laughing.
3-4s: THE GIRL does two small excited bunny-hops on the spot, landing back on the rug at real speed, pointing up at the reindeer trotting through the projected forest; THE BOY walks sideways past the ottoman with his head still tipped back, following the sleigh.
4-5s: both are still moving in the last frame - THE BOY still stepping and pointing up, THE GIRL trotting toward the Christmas tree while gazing up - never frozen.
They never stand still, never pose, never face the camera and never wait. Their feet stay on the floor: they never climb, jump onto or jump over the sofa, the ottoman or any furniture. Both stay in view: neither ducks behind furniture and pops up somewhere else. Real speed, real weight, grounded feet - no sliding, floating, slow motion or running in place. This child action never alters the camera movement.

CAMERA CONTINUITY: the entire five seconds are ONE slow, uninterrupted forward-and-upward glide. Forward travel and upward aim are coupled as one motion, already underway together in frame zero. Travel only about 0.8 metres forward over the entire shot, with an even upward tilt of about 18 degrees over those same five seconds. Wholesome family Christmas scene, children at home at Christmas.

TASK AND ASSETS: image-to-video, one continuous handheld phone POV take, vertical 9:16. The START IMAGE is frame 0.0s: a Williamsburg-blue painted six-panel Colonial door with raised panels, a satin finish and crisp white-painted surrounding trim fully closed with a wreath, a woman's hand with fair pale skin with pink undertones and a heather-grey Aran-knit wool sleeve on the brass knob. The ROOM REFERENCE IMAGE (attached as an image reference) controls only the room's architecture, furniture, colours and projected artwork; it contains no people and it is not an end frame, camera target or transition destination - never dissolve, cut, morph or snap into its composition. AUDIO - dry documentary sound only: the handle, the door swinging, the children gasping, laughing and calling out in delight, their footsteps on the floor, quiet room tone. (no BGM). No subtitles.

ROOM AND IDENTITY - a spacious double-height early-American Colonial Christmas great room with a high CATHEDRAL CEILING: two broad white plaster planes rise to a crisp central ridge, crossed by four honey-toned hand-hewn chestnut tie beams. Williamsburg blue-grey painted raised panelling lines the lower walls with white plaster above. A tall chimney breast of old red brick stands on the back wall with a white-painted wooden mantel; a wall of tall twelve-over-twelve sash windows with white-painted wooden frames and muntins occupies the left wall and a wide white-painted panelled doorway sits on the right. Wide heart-pine floorboards, a large oatmeal linen slipcovered sectional sofa, a round cognac leather ottoman and a red-and-cream braided wool rug.
THE BOY - a boy of about seven with fair skin and sandy-blond hair in a neat side part. THE GIRL - a girl of about four with fair rosy skin and light-brown hair in two little French braids. Both fully dressed in red-and-green Christmas tartan brushed-cotton pyjama sets with cream piping, with matching loose pyjama trousers and cream wool socks.

PROJECTED SCENE CONTENT - LOW ALONG THE WALLS, a SNOWY ENCHANTED CHRISTMAS FOREST: tall snow-laden fir trees hung with glowing lanterns, a herd of reindeer trotting between the trunks, a red fox family and snowshoe hares darting across the snowdrifts, a little log cabin with glowing windows and a curl of white smoke rising from its chimney, and a frozen stream winding through the trees. Along the upper walls, strings of golden lights, red velvet bows and dense emerald garlands swag from tree to tree into a continuous Christmas panorama, with big soft snowflakes drifting down through the branches. ACROSS THE CEILING, Santa's red sleigh and eight reindeer fly in a wide arc past a big silver full moon, among twinkling golden stars and shooting stars, a long sparkling golden trail streaming behind them. Unmistakably Christmas Eve, glowing gold, cranberry red and deep forest green dominant with snow-white accents against a near-black midnight sky. One dense continuous panorama of projected light across the architecture, no isolated sticker icons, no carnival, no galaxy, no vortex. No projected shape floats physically in the room.

BANNED - a third child or any extra people appearing at any moment; an adult; a child standing still, frozen, posed or waiting at any moment; the children standing in or next to the doorway when it opens; the children side by side or facing the camera; a child climbing onto or jumping on or over furniture; a child disappearing behind furniture and reappearing elsewhere; slow motion or floaty movement; a camera that moves, stops and moves again; a zoom; a cut; a glow along the door edge; light that grows or fills the room as the door opens; the projector unit visible; blur; music; subtitles.

MOTION GUARDRAILS - Failed if: the sleigh stays in one place or only its legs move; any chimney has no rising smoke; the reindeer herd, the foxes or the hares do not travel; the lanterns do not sway; snow does not fall; the picture moves only because the camera moves; the animation starts late, pauses or freezes at the end; the whole picture slides as one sheet; any projected shape floats in the room.

THE PROJECTOR - a single Christmas projector switched on and running in the room, out of shot. One lens throws one wide cone of light across the back wall and the whole vaulted ceiling.

LOW ALONG THE WALLS, a SNOWY ENCHANTED CHRISTMAS FOREST: tall snow-laden fir trees hung with glowing lanterns, a herd of reindeer trotting between the trunks, a red fox family and snowshoe hares darting across the snowdrifts, a little log cabin with glowing windows and a curl of white smoke rising from its chimney, and a frozen stream winding through the trees. Along the upper walls, strings of golden lights, red velvet bows and dense emerald garlands swag from tree to tree into a continuous Christmas panorama, with big soft snowflakes drifting down through the branches. ACROSS THE CEILING, Santa's red sleigh and eight reindeer fly in a wide arc past a big silver full moon, among twinkling golden stars and shooting stars, a long sparkling golden trail streaming behind them. Unmistakably Christmas Eve, glowing gold, cranberry red and deep forest green dominant with snow-white accents against a near-black midnight sky. One dense continuous panorama of projected light across the architecture, no isolated sticker icons, no carnival, no galaxy, no vortex. No projected shape floats physically in the room.

PROJECTED ANIMATION - THE MOST IMPORTANT THING IN THIS CLIP. The Christmas picture on the walls and the vaulted ceiling is a hand-drawn ANIMATED CHRISTMAS CARTOON playing at full speed, like a TV screen showing an animated Christmas movie - never a still picture. Only the projected picture is animated; the room and the children stay real photographic phone footage. Every drawn element below moves with big, bold, easy-to-read motion at normal playback speed, in EVERY frame from the first to the last, independent of the camera. Only the drawn buildings, trees and the moon stay put; everything else in the picture travels.
- SANTA'S SLEIGH AND EIGHT REINDEER: the WHOLE TEAM TRAVELS across the ceiling - it is never parked with only its legs moving. It flies in one smooth sweeping arc from where it is first seen across the vaulted ceiling toward the far side, covering about one quarter of the ceiling's width every second, so the sleigh is in a clearly different place every second and has crossed most of the ceiling by the end. The reindeer's legs gallop, heads bob, the sleigh rocks and dips, and the golden trail streams and curls behind it.
- FOREST LIFE: the herd of reindeer trots steadily between the tree trunks along the wall, legs moving and heads bobbing, travelling about one tree-width every second; the red foxes trot and pounce playfully in the snow; the snowshoe hares hop across the drifts; the snow-laden fir branches sway and shed little puffs of snow; the glowing lanterns sway gently on their branches and flicker; light glints travel along the frozen stream.
- CHIMNEY SMOKE: a tall soft white smoke plume streams continuously out of the log cabin's chimney, rising in a long wavy column up the wall, swaying and curling, each puff climbing about one cabin height every second, widening and fading as fresh puffs push up from below.
- SNOWFALL: big projected snowflakes drift steadily down across the whole picture, about a quarter of the wall height every second, turning as they fall - flat light on the wall and ceiling surfaces, never real snow in the room.
- SKY AND LIGHTS: the silver full moon glows; golden stars twinkle; now and then a shooting star streaks across the ceiling; bright gold sparkle waves travel along the garlands and ribbons.
PER-SECOND ANIMATION TRACK (4 s):
0-1s: the sleigh team is already flying, reindeer galloping; the reindeer herd is already trotting between the trees and smoke is already rising from the cabin chimney.
1-2s: the sleigh has moved a quarter of the ceiling and is crossing the silver moon; the reindeer herd trots on between the trunks; the cabin smoke climbs the wall; the foxes pounce.
2-3s: as the camera tilts up the sleigh flies on across the vaulted ceiling; the hares hop across the drifts; the lanterns sway; the fir branches shed puffs of snow.
3-4s: the sleigh keeps flying toward the far side of the ceiling; new smoke puffs rise from the cabin chimney; the reindeer herd keeps trotting; a shooting star streaks across - sleigh flying, reindeer herd trotting, cabin smoke rising, lanterns swaying, snow falling, all moving in the final frame.
All of this is flat projected light lying on the real plaster, the chestnut ridge beam and collar ties, the fieldstone chimney breast, the painted wainscot and the sash window frames: it bends with the surfaces, never floats into the room, and nothing is projected on the floor, furniture or children.

CHILDREN - AMAZED, AND ALWAYS MOVING:
THE GIRL lands from the jump shown in the start image onto the sofa cushions and keeps bouncing along the sofa with small, springy, real-speed bounces - her feet leave the cushion by only 15 to 25 centimetres, about two bounces per second, the cushion visibly sinking on every landing - moving a little further along the sofa with each bounce while she points one arm straight up at Santa's flying sleigh, head tipped back, mouth wide open, squealing with delight.
THE BOY walks slowly backwards across the rug with his head tipped far back, one arm stretched up pointing at the sleigh, turning his body to follow it as it flies across the ceiling; around 2 seconds he glances across at his sister, points up again and laughs, then keeps stepping and gazing up.
Neither child ever stands still, poses or faces the camera. Real children moving at real speed with real weight: quick, snappy bounces under normal gravity, never slow motion, never floating, never a high exaggerated leap, no morphing limbs.

TIMELINE (camera):
0.0-1.0s the person filming takes one small step forward; the frame moves in slightly and settles.
1.0-2.0s the camera holds in the closer position.
2.0-4.0s the camera tilts up in one slow smooth move past the top of the tree, only far enough that the vaulted ceiling fills the upper half of the frame - it never tilts past the projected picture. The ENTIRE vaulted ceiling in view, every plane, beam and corner, is covered with the projection - starry midnight sky, the silver full moon, the snowflakes and the flying sleigh - no bare plaster, no bare wood, no unprojected corner anywhere in view. Both children stay low in the frame and keep moving.
No zoom - the only forward movement is the one small physical step.

AUDIO: room tone, two children laughing, gasping and squealing, cushions thumping, feet on a rug. (no BGM). No subtitles.

SURFACE MAPPING - The two sloping plaster planes, the chestnut ridge beam and the collar ties remain stationary. The drawings fold at the ridge and run across the faces of the ridge beam and the collar ties, fold around the fieldstone chimney corners and carry across the white-painted window frames, muntins, wainscot and the Dutch door, with correct surface perspective and occlusion. All physical architecture stays stationary.

DO NOT INCLUDE: bare, blank, white or partly unprojected ceiling, beam, corner or wall in any frame, especially at the end when the camera looks up; a camera that tilts up beyond the projected picture; a projection appearing, fading in or revealing itself; children standing still; slow motion; floating or weightless movement; a third child; an adult; any music; the projector unit in shot; a zoom; blur; a cut; on-screen text; subtitles.

PROJECTED ANIMATION - THE MOST IMPORTANT THING IN THIS CLIP. The Christmas picture on the walls and the attic ceiling is a hand-drawn ANIMATED CHRISTMAS CARTOON playing at full speed, like a TV screen showing an animated Christmas movie - never a still picture. Only the projected picture is animated; the room and the children stay real photographic phone footage. Every drawn element below moves with big, bold, easy-to-read motion at normal playback speed, in EVERY frame from the first to the last, independent of the camera. Only the drawn buildings, trees and the moon stay put; everything else in the picture travels.
- SANTA'S SLEIGH AND EIGHT REINDEER: the WHOLE TEAM TRAVELS across the ceiling - it is never parked with only its legs moving. It flies in one smooth sweeping arc from where it is first seen across the attic ceiling toward the far side, covering about one quarter of the ceiling's width every second, so the sleigh is in a clearly different place every second and has crossed most of the ceiling by the end. The reindeer's legs gallop, heads bob, the sleigh rocks and dips, and the golden trail streams and curls behind it.
- CHIMNEY SMOKE - A HARD REQUIREMENT, NEVER SKIPPED: The log cabin and every other little house in the projected picture has a chimney on its roof, and from EVERY one of those chimneys a thick, bright white plume of smoke pours out continuously from the very first frame to the last - clearly visible against the dark night sky, never faint, never thin, never missing on any house. Each plume rises in a tall wavy column up the wall, swaying and curling, each puff climbing about one roof height every second, widening and fading higher up while fresh puffs keep pushing out of the chimney below. The smoke is always visibly rising. A house with a chimney but no rising smoke makes the clip wrong.
- FOREST LIFE: the herd of reindeer trots steadily between the tree trunks along the wall, legs moving and heads bobbing, travelling about one tree-width every second; the red foxes trot and pounce playfully in the snow; the snowshoe hares hop across the drifts; the snow-laden fir branches sway and shed little puffs of snow; the glowing lanterns sway gently on their branches and flicker; light glints travel along the frozen stream.
- SNOWFALL: big projected snowflakes drift steadily down across the whole picture, about a quarter of the wall height every second, turning as they fall - flat light on the wall and ceiling surfaces, never real snow in the room.
- SKY AND LIGHTS: the silver full moon glows; golden stars twinkle; now and then a shooting star streaks across the ceiling; bright gold sparkle waves travel along the garlands and ribbons.
PER-SECOND ANIMATION TRACK (while the camera tilts down):
0-1s: snow streams down the whole projection; the sleigh is already flying across the ceiling; the reindeer herd is already trotting between the trees and thick white smoke keeps pouring from the cabin chimney.
1-2s: the sleigh has flown a quarter of the way across the ceiling; the reindeer herd trots on between the trunks; the foxes pounce; thick white smoke keeps pouring from the cabin chimney.
2-3s: the sleigh flies on across the ceiling; the hares hop across the drifts; the lanterns sway; the fir branches shed puffs of snow.
3-4s: the sleigh keeps flying toward the far side of the ceiling; the reindeer herd keeps trotting; thick white smoke keeps pouring from the cabin chimney; a shooting star streaks across.
4-5s: sleigh flying, reindeer herd trotting, cabin smoke pouring, lanterns swaying, snow falling - all still moving in the last frame.
All of this is flat projected light lying on the real plaster, the pine collar beams, the painted panelling and the sash window frames: it bends with the surfaces, never floats into the room, and nothing is projected on the floor, the bed or the boy.

CAMERA - ONE slow, small tilt DOWN, the opposite of the other room clips in this set.
0.0s: the clip starts EXACTLY on the start image - the same framing, with the fully projected ceiling at the top of the frame. The camera never aims above this framing, so no part of the ceiling that is not in the start image ever appears, and every visible part of the ceiling is covered with the projection from the first frame - no bare plaster or bare wood anywhere.
0.0-4.0s: the camera tilts DOWN slowly and smoothly by about 10 degrees, like a calm parent filming: the projected ceiling slides up and partly out of the top of the frame while more of the bed, the covers and the rug come into view at the bottom. The boy stays fully in frame from head to feet.
4.0-5.0s: it eases to a gentle stop.
One continuous take: no cut, no zoom, no push-in, no pull-back, never a tilt up.

THE BOY HOPS HAPPILY ON HIS BED - natural, believable and unexaggerated, at real speed for all five seconds. Small, springy, continuous little hops on the same spot in the middle of the bed: his socks leave the duvet by only about 10 to 20 centimetres, about two hops per second, the bed giving softly on every landing. REAL-TIME SPEED, NOT SLOW MOTION: each hop is quick and snappy, taking only about half a second from push-off to landing under normal gravity, exactly like a real child filmed at normal speed - the footage is never slowed down, never time-stretched and never dreamy. Knees soft, head tipped back, mouth open, laughing up at the ceiling in amazement. While he hops he throws one arm straight up and points at Santa's sleigh, turning his head to follow it as it flies across the ceiling, then drops the arm and points again. He keeps the same size, face and clothes in every frame; he stays in the same spot on the bed. No high leaps, no hanging in the air, no floating, no hopping across the bed, no stiff movement, no morphing limbs.

Wholesome family Christmas home video: a happy, fully dressed little boy playing at home at Christmas while his mum films him. Crisp real iPhone footage at night, handheld, real-time speed, sharp from first frame to last.

ASSET ROLES: the start image, also attached as the image reference, is the approved still that controls a bright-proportioned early-American Colonial bedroom with a high ATTIC CEILING: two steep white plaster planes sloping up from the side walls to a flat central strip, crossed by honey-toned hand-hewn pine collar beams. A white-painted panelled bed with a wide upholstered oatmeal linen headboard is centred on the flat back wall between two tall twelve-over-twelve sash windows with white-painted wooden frames and muntins, Williamsburg blue-grey painted raised panelling on the lower back wall, white bedding with a red tartan wool throw, cherry bedside chests, wide heart-pine floorboards and a red-and-cream braided wool rug. Preserve the exact child identity, clothing, furniture, colours and the full projected artwork of the start image; its framing is the FIRST frame of this clip.

SUBJECTS:
THE BOY - exactly one child, a boy of about seven with fair skin and sandy-blond hair in a neat side part, fully dressed for winter in a long-sleeved red-and-green Christmas tartan brushed-cotton top with cream piping and matching loose lounge trousers, and cream wool socks, standing on the bed. No second child, no adult, no pet.
THE PROJECTOR - a single Christmas projector switched on and running on the bedroom floor, out of shot. One lens throws one wide cone of light across the walls and the whole attic ceiling. Everything glowing on the plaster and the wood is that one cone landing.
THE PICTURE - the animated scene is the exact Christmas panorama visible in the start image. LOW ALONG THE WALLS, a SNOWY ENCHANTED CHRISTMAS FOREST: tall snow-laden fir trees hung with glowing lanterns, a herd of reindeer trotting between the trunks, a red fox family and snowshoe hares darting across the snowdrifts, a little log cabin with glowing windows and a curl of white smoke rising from its chimney, and a frozen stream winding through the trees. Along the upper walls, strings of golden lights, red velvet bows and dense emerald garlands swag from tree to tree into a continuous Christmas panorama, with big soft snowflakes drifting down through the branches. ACROSS THE CEILING, Santa's red sleigh and eight reindeer fly in a wide arc past a big silver full moon, among twinkling golden stars and shooting stars, a long sparkling golden trail streaming behind them. Unmistakably Christmas Eve, glowing gold, cranberry red and deep forest green dominant with snow-white accents against a near-black midnight sky. One dense continuous panorama of projected light across the architecture, no isolated sticker icons, no carnival, no galaxy, no vortex. No projected shape floats physically in the room.

AUDIO: room tone, soft bumps on each landing, a boy laughing and gasping in delight. (no BGM). No subtitles.

DO NOT INCLUDE: a still, frozen or printed-looking projection; a projection that only twinkles; a sleigh parked in one place; chimneys without smoke; a cabin chimney without rising smoke; a reindeer herd, foxes or hares that do not travel; lanterns that do not sway; snow that does not fall; bare, white or unprojected ceiling or wall; a camera that starts higher than the start image; a camera that tilts up; zoom, push-in or pull-back; slow-motion or floaty jumping; high exaggerated leaps; the boy changing size or shape; real snow or particles in the air of the room; any music; the projector unit in shot; a second child; an adult; blur; a cut; on-screen text; subtitles.

````text
START FRAME LOCK - HIGHEST PRIORITY: frame 0.0s is EXACTLY the supplied start image, @Image 1 is the same picture. Keep its framing for the whole clip: the cream mural wall with the hand-painted Christmas tree, Santa and snowman running away on the right, the open doorway on the left, the man near the top of the orange stepladder on the left of the mural, and the woman sitting on the white drop cloth in the lower right. The camera never moves closer, never zooms in, never crops in and never reframes; the plain wall and ceiling at the top of the frame stay empty the whole time.

Real home video, shot on a hand-held iPhone by a person standing in the corner of the room, phone held upright at chest height. One continuous shot. The camera stays in exactly the same spot for the whole clip with only tiny natural hand sway and micro-jitter - the small live breathing wobble of real hand-held phone footage, never a steady locked-off tripod look. It does not reframe, does not follow him, does not tilt, pan, zoom or push in. No phone, hand, fingers, thumb or arm ever appears in the foreground.

HARD RULE, THIS ONE FIRST: the whole clip is ONE single unbroken shot from the first frame to the last. No cut, no jump cut, no second angle, no scene change, no transition, no dissolve at any point. The motion runs continuously - every moment grows out of the moment before it, and nobody's body ever resets, jumps position or teleports between one part and the next.

ACTION TIMELINE:
0.0-1.2s - He is painting the top of Santa's hat, brush in his right hand, the open tin of red paint in his left. She is painting a present at the bottom of the mural. Calm, nothing dramatic.

1.2-1.5s - Without any pause, he leans out to the right to reach further along the wall, stretching past the side of the ladder. His weight goes over, his foot slips off the step and the ladder starts to tip away from the wall to the left.

1.5-2.0s - He topples forward and down diagonally across the face of the mural towards the woman. His LEFT hand - the hand gripping the open red paint tin - drags down the wall as he falls, the open mouth of the tin pressed to the mural, and thick red paint pours out of the tin and runs down the wall in one long vertical red streak from where he was painting. His RIGHT hand holds only the brush and leaves no paint anywhere. The ladder falls over behind him onto the drop cloth.

2.0-2.4s - He lands hard, fast and heavy, face-down on the drop cloth at the foot of the mural wall, right beside the woman, his arms out in front of him. The ladder crashes down behind him. The tin hits the floor and a big splash of red paint bursts out across the drop cloth, spattering the lower wall.

2.4-4.0s - He lies still face-down, stunned. The tin rolls on its side across the drop cloth towards the camera, spilling a big glossy pool of red paint that spreads fast over the drop cloth in the foreground. The red streak keeps dripping down the wall. The woman has twisted round towards him on the drop cloth, both hands raised, mouth open in shock, staring at him. Red spatter on the wall, the drop cloth and her pyjamas.

The fall has real weight. An ordinary man's balance goes, gravity takes him, and he is on the floor in well under a second - fast, heavy, a bit ugly, exactly like real footage of someone coming off a ladder. His limbs are heavy and human, not floaty or rubbery, and he never hangs in the air. The paint is bright red acrylic house paint, thick and glossy, and behaves like real paint: it pours, drips, splashes and spreads in a flat puddle on the drop cloth. PAINT SOURCE: every drop of red paint in the clip comes out of the one open tin in his LEFT hand. No paint ever appears from his right hand, from the brush or out of nowhere; the streak on the wall starts exactly where the tin touches it.

No slow motion. No speed ramp, no speed change of any kind - the clip plays at one constant real-time speed from start to finish. No zoom, no push-in, no pan, no tilt. No captions or text.

Audio: room tone only, no music - the brush on the wall, the ladder scraping and crashing, a short yelp from him, the thud of his landing, the tin clanging and the paint splashing, then her shocked gasp.

FALL AND FRAMING LOCK - CHECK THIS LAST: he comes down right at the foot of the mural wall and lands on the drop cloth in front of it, right beside the woman, fully visible. He does not jump, step back or leap off the ladder, and he does not land behind her, on top of her or out of frame. The ladder and the tin stay inside the frame. The mural stays on the wall. The camera keeps the start image's framing to the very last frame; it never tilts down to follow his fall.

EVERY ELEMENT OF THE PROJECTION IS RAZOR SHARP: every elf, toy, ribbon, nutcracker detail, antler, snowflake, fir needle and window has precise edges. ACROSS THE CEILING, Santa's red sleigh and eight reindeer fly in a wide arc past a big silver full moon, among twinkling golden stars and shooting stars, a long sparkling golden trail streaming behind them. Unmistakably Christmas Eve, glowing gold, cranberry red and deep forest green dominant with snow-white accents against a near-black midnight sky. One dense continuous panorama of projected light across the architecture, no isolated sticker icons, no carnival, no galaxy, no vortex. No projected shape floats physically in the room. AND THE NIGHT SKY BETWEEN THE ELEMENTS IS DEEP AND DARK: everywhere the picture is not occupied by a lit element it is a rich near-black midnight sky of blackened indigo and very dark crimson, NOT white paint, NOT pale drywall and NOT a grey haze. It is that darkness between the elements that makes the bright ones burn.  HOW THE LIGHT SITS ON THE SURFACES, and this has to be exactly right. THE PROJECTION IS DENSE AND ROUGHLY NINETY PERCENT OPAQUE, and it is the brightest and most saturated thing in the frame. It lies on the room like an enormous backlit stained-glass window: deep, dense, fully saturated colour at full strength, crimson and ruby and warm gold and emerald at their richest, and it OVERPOWERS the colour of the surface underneath completely.  The material colour is overpowered by the projected colour; only surface texture and physical shadows remain legible through it. It is NOT pale, NOT pastel, NOT milky, NOT hazy, NOT semi-transparent, NOT a faint tint and NOT a washed-out overlay; there is no grey veil over any of it, and no part of the projection is so faint that the bare surface shows through as white.  AND IT WRAPS THE ARCHITECTURE. ONE SINGLE PROJECTOR sitting low in the near corner of the room throws all of this from that one point, so the entire picture keystones away from that origin and every surface takes it at its own angle. This is real projected light crossing a real three-dimensional room, so it obeys every surface it lands on.   The two steep sloping planes and the flat central strip remain clearly three-dimensional, meeting at crisp lines. The sleigh and the ceiling picture fold at each crease, are foreshortened along each slope and run straight across the faces of the pine collar beams before continuing down the walls. The flat wall, the painted raised panelling, the white-painted sash window frames and muntins and the linen headboard carry properly foreshortened portions of the same continuous projection across their faces. All raised trim casts a real hard-edged shadow behind it. THE WOODEN FRAMES CARRY THE PICTURE TOO: the painted wooden window frames, sashes and glazing bars, the door and doorway casings, the skirting boards, the mantel and every other piece of wooden trim the beam crosses are covered by the same continuous projected imagery as the wall beside them - never left as bare white unlit outlines - with only a thin real shadow line at each edge. THIS IS THE KEY CORRECTION FOR THIS SET: the exposed wood must visibly carry the picture. Every exposed piece of wood in shot - each beam, rafter, truss, brace, panel, post and rail, window frame, mullion, sash, muntin and glazing bar, the door and doorway casings, the skirting and the mantel - is painted over by the projection in the same full colour as the plaster beside it: the forest, lanterns, garlands, stars and snowflakes run straight across the face of the wood and bend at each edge, the wood grain showing faintly through the projected colour. No beam, frame or casing stays as a dark bare wooden stripe or an unlit outline. Only the window GLASS stays the dark night outside. There is NO falloff anywhere. The picture stays strong, dense and fully coloured right into all four corners of the frame and right up into the corners of the ceiling, with no dim zone, no dark gap, no faded edge and no bare patch at any boundary of the room.  EVERY ELEMENT OF THE PROJECTION IS RAZOR SHARP: every elf, toy, ribbon, nutcracker detail, antler, snowflake, fir needle and window has precise edges. The thousands of points of light are DISCRETE PINPOINTS - tiny, tight, individually separated dots - and not soft round blobs, not fuzzy balls and not smeared streaks. There is NO bloom, NO glow halo, NO lens flare, NO atmospheric haze, NO soft-focus glaze and NO gaussian softness anywhere over the picture, and the brightest cores stay tight and do not bleed into what surrounds them. The whole frame is in focus from the nearest person to the farthest corner of the ceiling: nothing is out of focus, nothing is soft, and there is no depth-of-field blur and no bokeh anywhere in the image.

CHANGE 4 - NOTHING IS PROJECTED ON THE FLOOR. The projected imagery lives on the WALLS and the CEILING only. The heart-pine floorboards, the red-and-cream braided wool rug, the bed and its covers, the bench at its foot and the little Christmas tree carry NO projected shapes at all - no snowflakes, no stars, no village, no Christmas figures, no gift village, no star, no sleigh, no falling light on them - they are lit only by the soft warm colour spill coming off the walls and sit in gentle shadow. The boy's face and his cotton pyjama top catch that spill so his face reads clearly.

CHANGE 5 - the projector unit must NOT be visible. No projector, no stool, no side table, no glowing device anywhere in frame. The corner it would occupy is plain wall and floor.

NATURAL, LESS CINEMATIC LOOK - one small notch more natural than a polished film still: this is a candid photo a parent snapped on an iPhone at home, not a movie frame, a poster, a painting or an advert. True-to-life colour and contrast exactly as they come straight out of the phone camera: no colour grading, no teal-and-orange grade, no vignette, no dramatic spotlighting, no heightened fantasy glow on the children, and no storybook-illustration or oil-painting finish on the projection. The projection is still dense, bright and colourful, but its colour sits a touch closer to real life - rich, not neon - and it reads as the real light of a home Christmas projector falling on real walls: the plaster texture and wood grain show faintly through it, and the shadows keep a little more ordinary room detail, with the furniture, rug and floor clearly readable in the soft spill.

Vertical 9:16 photo, shot on an iPhone 16 Pro, natural handheld UGC look, extremely high quality, RAZOR SHARP EDGE TO EDGE AND CORNER TO CORNER at full 2K resolution with fine micro-detail legible everywhere - the fine cotton weave of the pyjamas, the grain of the floorboards, the tooth of the plaster, the crisp architectural edges of the ceiling and every individual pinpoint of the projection all crisply resolved - perfect lighting, perfectly exposed, hyper realistic, zero motion blur, zero depth-of-field blur, zero bokeh, zero bloom, zero glow haze, zero soft focus, nothing blurry, nothing smeared and nothing soft or gauzy anywhere in the frame, no CGI look. Normal wide-angle lens, straight vertical lines, no fisheye distortion.

No text, no captions, no watermarks, no lettering anywhere in the image.
